# SVC metrics — 20 Mbit/s with ±20 ms jitter (2026-11-04)

What was run: the pre-registered SVC metrics protocol on one more link, 20 Mbit/s with ±20 ms of jitter each way, on three volumes, 13 variants, at full and quarter CPU speed.
What it shows: *the sitting is in progress, 2 of 10 rounds done; the reading is written when it is whole.*
What it rests on: three digital breast tomosynthesis (DBT) volumes only, `dbts_a1`, `dbts_b1` and `dbts_c1`; one 4-core container; headless Chromium 153.0.8010.12. Nothing is claimed for any other series, modality, host or device.
What is left: the breast ultrasound cine and ABUS rows, and the 5 and 50 Mbit/s and 2 % loss links, which other sittings run; the rule's verdict, which another session applies once every condition is in.

*The tables are partial until round 10; rows are pushed after every round.*

The protocol is [`docs/av1/README.md`](../../../../../docs/av1/README.md) §The scalable preview as a tool, part 10; how each
number is made is [`README.md`](README.md) §SVC metrics; the first sitting (no rate limit, and 20 Mbit/s without
jitter) is [`svc-results.md`](svc-results.md), whose method this sitting keeps: the same commands, with
`--links r20000 --impairs j20` in place of `--links none,r20000`, the cells and the variants inside each in
`run.mjs`'s Williams order (`lab/order.mjs`), a fresh browser a visit, 10 whole rounds. The layer errors (metric 3)
and the offline decode (metric 5's second half) do not depend on the link and were not re-run: they are the first
sitting's.

**Terms.** A *variant* is one way of coding and sending the volume (the protocol's table names all 13). *SVC*
(scalable video coding) codes a frame as layers: a quarter-size lossy *base*, for `p3-*` a full-size lossy *middle*,
and the *exact* (lossless) frame on top. *Layer-major*: every base is sent first, then every middle, then every exact
frame. *t_X* is the time from the fill's issue until every slice is exact; *t_P* until every slice has a picture.
*1× and 4×* are the browser's CPU unthrottled and slowed fourfold (`lab/scripts/cpu_throttle.mjs`). A *cell* is one
volume × link × CPU. A visit is *`VOID`* when the relay that emulates the link sent its p99 packet more than 1 ms
late (it timed itself), and is then dropped and counted. *A/A*: HTJ2K sent twice (`htj2k`, `htj2k-aa`); their
difference is the resolution below which nothing is reported.

**The link, and how the jitter was applied.** `run.mjs`'s `r20000` with impairment `j20`: the userspace relay
`lab/scripts/link_impair.py`, 20 Mbit/s each way through a 200-packet tail-drop queue, 20 ms one way, plus
`--jitter-ms 20 --jitter-mode ordered`: each packet's one-way delay drawn uniform in 20 ± 20 ms after the rate queue,
then clamped so that no packet leaves before the one ahead of it (one radio leg delivers in order;
[`docs/rig-limits.md`](../../../../../docs/rig-limits.md) §3). The draw is per packet; real jitter's shape, correlated
over milliseconds, is not modelled. The container has no `tc`, so nothing is shaped in the kernel.

**Checked on the wire.** [`wire_delay.py`](wire_delay.py) reads every UDP datagram into and out of the relay off
loopback (an `AF_PACKET` socket, the server's GSO batches split at their `gso_size`) and pairs each by payload. One
`k2` visit on `dbts_a1` at 1× through each link, sniffed for 25 s:

| link | direction | datagrams | transit, ms: min · p10 · p50 · p90 · max | out of order |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `r20000`, no jitter | client → server (acks; no queue) | 4 546 | 14.8 · 20.1 · 20.2 · 20.3 · 60.1 | 0 |
| | server → client (data; the queue standing) | 41 124 | 19.7 · 89.3 · 111.4 · 127.7 · 168.7 | 0 |
| `r20000-j20` | client → server | 4 653 | 0.4 · 15.9 · 28.1 · 37.8 · 58.8 | 0 |
| | server → client | 40 882 | 3.9 · 92.5 · 120.9 · 139.9 · 156.8 | 0 |

So the jitter is on the wire: unqueued, a delay of 20.1–20.3 ms (p10–p90) becomes 15.9–37.8 ms, reaching down to
0.4 ms, with nothing overtaken. The median rises above 20 ms because the in-order clamp holds a packet behind a later
draw ahead of it (*derived* from the relay's code; with sparse probes the median stays 20.3). The data direction is
mostly the queue's 90–140 ms; there the jitter shows in the floor (3.9 ms against 19.7). Under the sniffer both
visits were `VOID` (relay p99 1.18–1.19 ms; the tap adds work to the relay's own sends), so they are not rows. The
check was mutated: probes 5 ms apart through `--jitter-mode reorder` came back 144 of 397 out of order, through
`ordered` none; at 50 ms apart, ±20 ms gave 0.3–39.5 ms against 20.0–24.7 with no jitter.

**Host and where it saturates.** 4 vCPU, 15 GB: browser and server on three cores, the relay alone on the fourth,
as the first sitting. At 20 Mbit/s every exact fill arrives at the link's rate (the per-cell received rate below,
2.43–2.44 MB/s against the link's 2.5), so these cells are link-bound and do not reach the CPU's limit. The relay is
the part that strains: its self-timing p99 is higher with jitter than the first sitting's 20 Mbit/s without
(*see the control pair below*), so more visits are `VOID`.

**The control pair.** After each round, one `k2` visit on `dbts_a1` at 1× without jitter and one with, in
alternating order, kept out of the rows and the tables: whether the `VOID`s come from the jitter or the host.

**Exactness**, every visit, `VOID` ones included: each exact frame against its checksum, each base and middle against
native dav1d at its operating point, no preview after its frame's exact picture, no stray (the per-cell lines
below). These checks were mutated in the first sitting ([`svc-mutations.jsonl`](svc-mutations.jsonl)) on the same
code, and not again here; the one new check, the wire's, was mutated above.

## Tables

Visits 156, kept 121, VOID 35; cells 6.

## Rows
